## Plan (verbatim)

# Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling

## Overview

Goal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. Build a stdlib HTTP server with `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup` (PAT-mode only, bound to `127.0.0.1:8765`, CORS-enabled, Bearer-auth on the two state-changing endpoints); teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` paths inside the materialization dir; and **make cleanup actually fire via a periodic age-based janitor**, not via per-task hooks that break sibling-task sharing.

**Architecture decision driven by iter-3 critique** (resolves FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations):
The shipped app's `create-task/index.ts:502-504` copies the same `materialized_inputs` array onto every task returned by a resolver, and `maskedEditResolver:83-85` creates one queued task per `num_generations`. Multiple non-orchestrator sibling tasks share the same `file://` input. The v3 plan's per-task self-cleanup would delete that shared input after the first sibling completes, breaking the rest. Switching to a **periodic age-based janitor** fixes this in one move:
- Background daemon thread sweeps the materialization dir every N minutes (default 30 min) and deletes files whose mtime is older than the configured TTL (default 6 hours).
- All sibling tasks of a single `create-task` call finish well within 6h, so shared inputs survive long enough to be consumed.
- All failure branches (raw-result failure, unhandled exception, requeue exhaustion) are covered uniformly without per-branch hooking — addresses FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations.
- Orchestrator chains (travel/join/edit_video) typically finish within the TTL; if a chain runs longer, the worker can extend the TTL via env var.
- Sweep also runs ONCE at startup so files left from a previous crash get cleaned.

The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays as a contract entry-point (built, tested, idempotent, auth-gated) for any future browser-driven flow that wants to explicitly free a file early. Cleanup correctness no longer depends on it.

**Other course corrections from iter-3 critique:**
- **FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2** (auth tradeoff visibility): keep `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` (default off) for single-machine smoke testing, but make the tradeoff loud: emit an `headless_logger.warning(...)` line at startup whenever the knob is enabled, prefixed `[SECURITY]`, naming the brief's threat model and instructing production setups to leave it unset. Add the same warning to the deliverable summary.

Repo shape:
- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`); auth-mode resolver at `server.py:47-64`.
- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:202-240`).
- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64`.
- Test framework: `pytest` (`pyproject.toml:150-158`); `requests` already a dep.

Constraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; refuse path-traversal AND literal `..` components; gate startup on PAT-mode; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port`; do NOT pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int, worker_id: str, version: str, auth_optional: bool = False, file_ttl_seconds: int = 21600, janitor_interval_seconds: int = 1800) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.
2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `("127.0.0.1", port)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass.
3. **Endpoints**:
   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{"ok": true, "worker_id": ..., "version": ...}`. No auth check.
   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time `hmac.compare_digest`) UNLESS `auth_optional=True`. Parse `multipart/form-data` with `cgi.FieldStorage` reading exactly one file part named `file`. Sanitize the upload's filename suffix via `re.search(r"\\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$", filename)`; default `.bin`. Write to `materialization_dir / f"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}"` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`. Respond `200` with `{"path": "<abs>", "size": <bytes>}`.
   - `POST /cleanup` → same auth gate. Read JSON `{"path": "..."}`. **Validate path components**: if any part of `Path(payload_path).parts` equals `".."`, return `400` (literal `..` ban — addresses prior FLAG-005). Then resolve via `Path(path).resolve(strict=False)` and require `is_relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())`. Outside → `400`. Doesn't exist → `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.
   - `OPTIONS <any>` → `204` with CORS headers.
   - Any other path/method → `404`.
   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug`.
4. **CORS**: on every response, set `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, OPTIONS`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`, `Access-Control-Max-Age: 600`.
5. **Token + persisted state**: `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`. Ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists with mode 0700; write `auth-token` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actual `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On shutdown, best-effort delete both files.
6. **Materialization dir**: `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True, parents=True)`; on POSIX, `os.chmod(0o700)` if pre-existed (best-effort).
7. **Threading**: `serve_forever()` on a daemon HTTP thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()`, `server.server_close()`, joins with 5-second timeout AND signals the janitor thread to exit (see Step 2).

### Step 2: Add the periodic age-based janitor (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Small — addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations

1. **Add** a `_FileJanitor` class in the same module:
   - Constructor takes `materialization_dir: Path`, `file_ttl_seconds: int`, `interval_seconds: int`, plus a `threading.Event` `stop_event`.
   - `run()` method: at startup do one `_sweep()` immediately (clean up files left over from prior crashes); then loop `while not stop_event.wait(interval_seconds): self._sweep()`.
   - `_sweep()` method: iterate `materialization_dir.iterdir()`, skip non-files, compute `age = time.time() - file.stat().st_mtime`. If `age > file_ttl_seconds`, `unlink(missing_ok=True)`. Wrap each unlink in `try/except OSError` and log via `headless_logger.debug`. NEVER follow symlinks; use `Path.is_file()` (which returns False for symlinks pointing outside, since `iterdir` only returns immediate children of the resolved dir). Skip any file whose name doesn't look like our `<uuid>.<ext>` pattern (regex `^[a-f0-9]{32}\\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$`) — keeps the janitor safe if a user manually drops files into the dir.
2. **Run** the janitor on its own daemon thread, started inside `start_local_http_server`. Pass the same `stop_event` shared with the HTTP server's `shutdown()`. On `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`, set the event, join the janitor thread with a 5-second timeout.
3. **Why this design satisfies all open cleanup flags**:
   - Sibling tasks sharing a `file://` input all finish within `file_ttl_seconds=6h` → janitor doesn't race them.
   - All failure branches (raw-result failure path `server.py:740-757`, unhandled-exception path `server.py:760-789`, exhausted-retry, orchestrator chains) are covered uniformly with zero per-branch hooks.
   - Crash recovery: stale files from a prior crash get swept on next startup.
   - Worker doesn't need DB queries to check sibling-task references.

### Step 3: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** the helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (`server.py:47-64`):
   ```python
   def _is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, *, access_token: str | None) -> bool:
       service_key = os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY")
       if service_key:
           return False
       if os.environ.get("WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE", "").strip().lower() == "service":
           return False
       return bool(access_token)
   ```
2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, access_token=access_token)`.
3. **Resolve** env-driven config:
   - `port = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT", "8765"))`
   - `mat_dir = Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))).expanduser()`
   - `auth_optional = os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL") in ("1", "true", "yes")`
   - `file_ttl_seconds = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_FILE_TTL_SECONDS", "21600"))` (6h default)
   - `janitor_interval_seconds = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_JANITOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS", "1800"))` (30min default)
4. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()`, defaulting to `None`. In the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guard with `if local_http_server:` and wrap shutdown in `try/except BaseException`.
5. **Bind-failure posture**: if `start_local_http_server` raises `OSError` (port-in-use), log `headless_logger.warning(...)` and continue without the HTTP surface. Polling worker stays functional in cloud-fallback mode.
6. **Log** at startup:
   - `headless_logger.essential(f"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port}, materializing to {mat_dir} (TTL={file_ttl_seconds}s, sweep every {janitor_interval_seconds}s)")`
   - **If `auth_optional=True`** (addresses FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2): `headless_logger.warning(f"[SECURITY] REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1 — /ingest and /cleanup accept requests WITHOUT Authorization. The brief's localhost-attacker threat model is opt-out in this configuration. Production worker setups must leave this knob unset.")`
7. **Do NOT add per-task cleanup hooks to `server.py`** (the v3 hooks at `:737-738` and `:740-757` are removed from the plan). The janitor covers all paths uniformly.

### Step 4: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206`. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:
   - If `parsed_url.scheme == "file"`:
     - **Reject** if any component of `Path(parsed_url.path).parts` equals `".."` → `DownloadError`.
     - Resolve with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`; require `is_relative_to(_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve())`, `exists()`, `is_file()`.
     - On success: return the resolved path string without copying.
     - On failure: raise `DownloadError(f"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}")`.
   - Existing `http`/`https` branch unchanged.
2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))).expanduser()`. Read each call (no cache).
3. **Coverage**: `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` both delegate to `_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:248-277`); single change covers both.
4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` early-return alone; `file://` is handled before that branch.

### Step 6: Tests for the janitor (`tests/test_janitor.py`)
**Scope:** Small — addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations

1. **Create** `tests/test_janitor.py`. Drive `_FileJanitor._sweep()` directly without a thread for deterministic assertions.
2. **Cover**:
   - `test_sweep_deletes_files_older_than_ttl` — create file, set mtime to `now - 7h` (TTL 6h), sweep, file gone.
   - `test_sweep_keeps_fresh_files` — fresh file (mtime = now), sweep, file present.
   - `test_sweep_keeps_files_at_exactly_ttl` — borderline (mtime = now - ttl), sweep, kept (use `>` not `>=`).
   - `test_sweep_skips_unrecognized_filenames` — drop a file named `random.txt` (doesn't match `<uuid>.<ext>` pattern); sweep; file still present even when stale (safety net for user-dropped files).
   - `test_sweep_handles_unlink_oserror_gracefully` — patch `Path.unlink` to raise `OSError`; sweep doesn't propagate.
   - `test_startup_sweep_cleans_stale_files` — start the janitor with `interval_seconds=99999`; immediately observe stale files were deleted by the startup sweep; then `stop_event.set()` and join.
   - `test_sweep_does_not_follow_symlinks_outside_dir` — create a symlink inside materialization dir pointing to a file outside; ensure janitor does not follow it (file outside is untouched).

### Step 8: Document the integration + security tradeoffs (no app-side code change)
**Scope:** Small

1. **In the deliverable summary**, enumerate:
   - **FOLLOW-UP A (auth, recommended long-term)**: shipped `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/ingest.ts` does not send `Authorization`. Long-term fix: app reads `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` and sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Short-term unblock: deployer sets `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` on the worker.
   - **SECURITY NOTE on auth-optional** (addresses FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2): `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` is **opt-out from the brief's documented threat model** (any local process on the same machine can hit `/ingest` and `/cleanup` to materialize files or delete legitimate files). It is intended for single-machine dev smoke testing only. The worker emits `[SECURITY]` warning logs at startup when the knob is enabled. Production worker setups MUST leave it unset; once FOLLOW-UP A Option 1 lands, the knob is no longer needed.
   - **FOLLOW-UP B (cleanup)**: not blocking. Worker runs a periodic janitor that deletes materialized files older than `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_FILE_TTL_SECONDS` (default 6h) every `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_JANITOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 30 min). Works for sibling tasks that share materialized inputs (`create-task/index.ts:502-504` + `maskedEditResolver:83-85` pattern), all failure paths uniformly, and crash recovery. The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays as a contract entry-point if the app ever wants to free a file early.
   - **FOLLOW-UP C (port, optional)**: app reads `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for non-default ports. Worker writes it; app currently uses hardcoded `8765` and we match.
   - **WITHDRAWN suggestions**: the v2 "edge function calls requestLocalWorkerCleanup" idea is mechanically incoherent (Deno hosted edge cannot reach `localhost`) and is withdrawn. The v3 per-task self-cleanup design is withdrawn because it breaks `num_generations`-style sibling tasks that share a `file://` input.
2. **Do not edit** the app side.

  raise\n+\n+\n+def _make_handler(\n+    *,\n+    materialization_dir: Path,\n+    worker_id: str,\n+    version: str,\n+    token: str,\n+    auth_optional: bool,\n+) -> type[BaseHTTPRequestHandler]:\n+    resolved_materialization_dir = materialization_dir.resolve()\n+\n+    class LocalWorkerRequestHandler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):\n+        server_version = \"ReighLocalWorkerHTTP/1.0\"\n+\n+        def do_OPTIONS(self) -> None:\n+            self._send_empty(HTTPStatus.NO_CONTENT)\n+\n+        def do_GET(self) -> None:\n+            if self.path != self.path == \"/cleanup\":\n+                self._handle_cleanup()\n+                return\n+            self._send_json(HTTPStatus.NOT_FOUND, {\"error\": \"not found\"})\n+\n+        def log_message(self, format: str, *args: Any) -> None:\n+            headless_logger.debug(f\"Local HTTP {self.address_string()} - {format % args}\")\n+\n+        def _handle_ingest(self) -> None:\n+            if not self._is_authorized():\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.UNAUTHORIZED, {\"error\": \"unauthorized\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            content_type = self.headers.get(\"Content-Type\", \"\")\n+            if \"multipart/form-data\" not in content_type:\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, {\"error\": \"expected multipart/form-data\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            form = cgi.FieldStorage(\n+                fp=self.rfile,\n+                headers=self.headers,\n+                environ={\n+                    \"REQUEST_METHOD\": \"POST\",\n+                    \"CONTENT_TYPE\": content_type,\n+                    \"CONTENT_LENGTH\": self.headers.get(\"Content-Length\", \"0\"),\n+                },\n+            )\n+            file_field = form[\"file\"] if \"file\" in form else None\n+            if isinstance(file_field, list) or file_field is None or not getattr(file_field, \"file\", None):\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, {\"error\": \"expected exactly one file part named file\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            filename = getattr(file_field, \"filename\", \"\") or \"\"\n+            suffix_match = _UPLOAD_SUFFIX_RE.search(filename)\n+            suffix = suffix_match.group(0) if suffix_match else \".bin\"\n+            output_path = materialization_dir / f\"{uuid.uuid4().hex}{suffix}\"\n+\n+            fd = os.open(output_path, os.O_WRONLY | os.O_CREAT | os.O_EXCL, 0o600)\n+            size = 0\n+            try:\n+                with os.fdopen(fd, \"wb\") as handle:\n+                    while True:\n+                        chunk = file_field.file.read(1024 * 1024)\n+                        if not chunk:\n+                            break\n+                        handle.write(chunk)\n+                        size += len(chunk)\n+            except Exception:\n+                try:\n+                    output_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)\n+                except OSError as exc:\n+                    headless_logger.debug(f\"Failed to clean partial ingest file {output_path}: {exc}\")\n+                raise\n+\n+            self._send_json(HTTPStatus.OK, {\"path\": str(output_path.resolve()), \"size\": size})\n+\n+        def _handle_cleanup(self) -> None:\n+            if not self._is_authorized():\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.UNAUTHORIZED, {\"error\": \"unauthorized\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            try:\n+                content_length = int(self.headers.get(\"Content-Length\", \"0\"))\n+            except ValueError:\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, {\"error\": \"invalid content length\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            try:\n+                payload = json.loads(self.rfile.read(content_length).decode(\"utf-8\"))\n+            except (UnicodeDecodeError, json.JSONDecodeError):\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, {\"error\": \"invalid json\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            payload_path = payload.get(\"path\") if isinstance(payload, dict) else None\n+            if not isinstance(payload_path, str):\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, {\"error\": \"path is required\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            candidate = Path(payload_path)\n+            if any(part == \"..\" for part in candidate.parts):\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, {\"error\": \"path not allowed\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            resolved_candidate = candidate.resolve(strict=False)\n+            if not resolved_candidate.is_relative_to(resolved_materialization_dir):\n+                self._send_json(HTTPStatus.BAD_REQUEST, {\"error\": \"path not allowed\"})\n+                return\n+\n+            if not resolved_candidate.exists():\n+                self._send_empty(HTTPStatus.NO_CONTENT)\n+                return\n+\n+            try:\n+                resolved_candidate.unlink()\n+            except FileNotFoundError:\n+                pass\n+            self._send_empty(HTTPStatus.NO_CONTENT)\n+\n+        def _is_authorized(self) -> bool:\n+            if auth_optional:\n+                return True\n+            auth_header = self.headers.get(\"Authorization\", \"\")\n+            prefix = \"Bearer \"\n+            if not auth_header.startswith(prefix):\n+                return False\n+            return hmac.compare_digest(auth_header[len(prefix) :], token)\n+\n+        def _send_json(self, status: HTTPStatus, payload: dict[str, Any]) -> None:\n+            body = json.dumps(payload).encode(\"utf-8\")\n+            self.send_response(status)\n+            self._send_cors_headers()\n+            self.send_header(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n+            self.send_header(\"Content-Length\", str(len(body)))\n+            self.end_headers()\n+            self.wfile.write(body)\n+\n+        def _send_empty(self, status: HTTPStatus) -> None:\n+            self.send_response(status)\n+            self._send_cors_headers()\n+            self.send_header(\"Content-Length\", \"0\")\n+            self.end_headers()\n+\n+        def _send_cors_headers(self) -> None:\n+            self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\", \"*\")\n+            self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Allow-Methods\", \"GET, POST, OPTIONS\")\n+            self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Allow-Headers\", \"Authorization, Content-Type\")\n+            self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Max-Age\", \"600\")\n+\n+    return LocalWorkerRequestHandler\n*** End Patch\n"
}

os.environ['REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR'] = str(tmp_path / 'mat')\n    mat = tmp_path / 'mat'\n    srv = start_local_http_server(materialization_dir=mat, port=0, worker_id='worker-a', version='test')\n    port = srv.server.server_address[1]\n    base = f'http://127.0.0.1:{port}'\n    try:\n        r = requests.get(f'{base}/health', timeout=5)\n        assert r.status_code == 200, r.text\n        assert port = srv.server.server_address[1]\n    base = f'http://127.0.0.1:{port}'\n    try:\n        r = requests.get(f'{base}/health', timeout=5)\n        assert r.status_code == 200, r.text\n        assert # Execute this implementation plan EXACTLY

This is a pre-refined plan that already went through 4 rounds of critique-and-revise. The architecture and approach are settled. Your job is to **execute it**, not re-plan it.

Constraints for the planning phase:
- Treat the plan below as the authoritative spec.
- Do NOT propose alternative architectures.
- Do NOT add scope.
- Do NOT add comments explaining the change.
- If you find a step ambiguous, pick the simplest interpretation and proceed; flag it in deviations rather than blocking.
- Reuse existing helpers; don't introduce new abstractions beyond what the plan calls for.

Tests must cover the cases listed at the end of the plan. After the change, run `pytest` (or whatever the project's test framework is — see `pyproject.toml`).

## Plan (verbatim)

# Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling

## Overview

Goal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. Build a stdlib HTTP server with `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup` (PAT-mode only, bound to `127.0.0.1:8765`, CORS-enabled, Bearer-auth on the two state-changing endpoints); teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` paths inside the materialization dir; and **make cleanup actually fire via a periodic age-based janitor**, not via per-task hooks that break sibling-task sharing.

**Architecture decision driven by iter-3 critique** (resolves FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations):
The shipped app's `create-task/index.ts:502-504` copies the same `materialized_inputs` array onto every task returned by a resolver, and `maskedEditResolver:83-85` creates one queued task per `num_generations`. Multiple non-orchestrator sibling tasks share the same `file://` input. The v3 plan's per-task self-cleanup would delete that shared input after the first sibling completes, breaking the rest. Switching to a **periodic age-based janitor** fixes this in one move:
- Background daemon thread sweeps the materialization dir every N minutes (default 30 min) and deletes files whose mtime is older than the configured TTL (default 6 hours).
- All sibling tasks of a single `create-task` call finish well within 6h, so shared inputs survive long enough to be consumed.
- All failure branches (raw-result failure, unhandled exception, requeue exhaustion) are covered uniformly without per-branch hooking — addresses FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations.
- Orchestrator chains (travel/join/edit_video) typically finish within the TTL; if a chain runs longer, the worker can extend the TTL via env var.
- Sweep also runs ONCE at startup so files left from a previous crash get cleaned.

The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays as a contract entry-point (built, tested, idempotent, auth-gated) for any future browser-driven flow that wants to explicitly free a file early. Cleanup correctness no longer depends on it.

**Other course corrections from iter-3 critique:**
- **FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2** (auth tradeoff visibility): keep `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` (default off) for single-machine smoke testing, but make the tradeoff loud: emit an `headless_logger.warning(...)` line at startup whenever the knob is enabled, prefixed `[SECURITY]`, naming the brief's threat model and instructing production setups to leave it unset. Add the same warning to the deliverable summary.

Repo shape:
- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`); auth-mode resolver at `server.py:47-64`.
- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:202-240`).
- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64`.
- Test framework: `pytest` (`pyproject.toml:150-158`); `requests` already a dep.

Constraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; refuse path-traversal AND literal `..` components; gate startup on PAT-mode; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port`; do NOT pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int, worker_id: str, version: str, auth_optional: bool = False, file_ttl_seconds: int = 21600, janitor_interval_seconds: int = 1800) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.
2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `("127.0.0.1", port)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass.
3. **Endpoints**:
   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{"ok": true, "worker_id": ..., "version": ...}`. No auth check.
   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time `hmac.compare_digest`) UNLESS `auth_optional=True`. Parse `multipart/form-data` with `cgi.FieldStorage` reading exactly one file part named `file`. Sanitize the upload's filename suffix via `re.search(r"\\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$", filename)`; default `.bin`. Write to `materialization_dir / f"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}"` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`. Respond `200` with `{"path": "<abs>", "size": <bytes>}`.
   - `POST /cleanup` → same auth gate. Read JSON `{"path": "..."}`. **Validate path components**: if any part of `Path(payload_path).parts` equals `".."`, return `400` (literal `..` ban — addresses prior FLAG-005). Then resolve via `Path(path).resolve(strict=False)` and require `is_relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())`. Outside → `400`. Doesn't exist → `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.
   - `OPTIONS <any>` → `204` with CORS headers.
   - Any other path/method → `404`.
   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug`.
4. **CORS**: on every response, set `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, OPTIONS`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`, `Access-Control-Max-Age: 600`.
5. **Token + persisted state**: `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`. Ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists with mode 0700; write `auth-token` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actual `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On shutdown, best-effort delete both files.
6. **Materialization dir**: `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True, parents=True)`; on POSIX, `os.chmod(0o700)` if pre-existed (best-effort).
7. **Threading**: `serve_forever()` on a daemon HTTP thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()`, `server.server_close()`, joins with 5-second timeout AND signals the janitor thread to exit (see Step 2).

### Step 2: Add the periodic age-based janitor (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Small — addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations

1. **Add** a `_FileJanitor` class in the same module:
   - Constructor takes `materialization_dir: Path`, `file_ttl_seconds: int`, `interval_seconds: int`, plus a `threading.Event` `stop_event`.
   - `run()` method: at startup do one `_sweep()` immediately (clean up files left over from prior crashes); then loop `while not stop_event.wait(interval_seconds): self._sweep()`.
   - `_sweep()` method: iterate `materialization_dir.iterdir()`, skip non-files, compute `age = time.time() - file.stat().st_mtime`. If `age > file_ttl_seconds`, `unlink(missing_ok=True)`. Wrap each unlink in `try/except OSError` and log via `headless_logger.debug`. NEVER follow symlinks; use `Path.is_file()` (which returns False for symlinks pointing outside, since `iterdir` only returns immediate children of the resolved dir). Skip any file whose name doesn't look like our `<uuid>.<ext>` pattern (regex `^[a-f0-9]{32}\\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$`) — keeps the janitor safe if a user manually drops files into the dir.
2. **Run** the janitor on its own daemon thread, started inside `start_local_http_server`. Pass the same `stop_event` shared with the HTTP server's `shutdown()`. On `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`, set the event, join the janitor thread with a 5-second timeout.
3. **Why this design satisfies all open cleanup flags**:
   - Sibling tasks sharing a `file://` input all finish within `file_ttl_seconds=6h` → janitor doesn't race them.
   - All failure branches (raw-result failure path `server.py:740-757`, unhandled-exception path `server.py:760-789`, exhausted-retry, orchestrator chains) are covered uniformly with zero per-branch hooks.
   - Crash recovery: stale files from a prior crash get swept on next startup.
   - Worker doesn't need DB queries to check sibling-task references.

### Step 3: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** the helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (`server.py:47-64`):
   ```python
   def _is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, *, access_token: str | None) -> bool:
       service_key = os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY")
       if service_key:
           return False
       if os.environ.get("WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE", "").strip().lower() == "service":
           return False
       return bool(access_token)
   ```
2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, access_token=access_token)`.
3. **Resolve** env-driven config:
   - `port = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT", "8765"))`
   - `mat_dir = Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))).expanduser()`
   - `auth_optional = os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL") in ("1", "true", "yes")`
   - `file_ttl_seconds = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_FILE_TTL_SECONDS", "21600"))` (6h default)
   - `janitor_interval_seconds = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_JANITOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS", "1800"))` (30min default)
4. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()`, defaulting to `None`. In the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guard with `if local_http_server:` and wrap shutdown in `try/except BaseException`.
5. **Bind-failure posture**: if `start_local_http_server` raises `OSError` (port-in-use), log `headless_logger.warning(...)` and continue without the HTTP surface. Polling worker stays functional in cloud-fallback mode.
6. **Log** at startup:
   - `headless_logger.essential(f"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port}, materializing to {mat_dir} (TTL={file_ttl_seconds}s, sweep every {janitor_interval_seconds}s)")`
   - **If `auth_optional=True`** (addresses FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2): `headless_logger.warning(f"[SECURITY] REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1 — /ingest and /cleanup accept requests WITHOUT Authorization. The brief's localhost-attacker threat model is opt-out in this configuration. Production worker setups must leave this knob unset.")`
7. **Do NOT add per-task cleanup hooks to `server.py`** (the v3 hooks at `:737-738` and `:740-757` are removed from the plan). The janitor covers all paths uniformly.

### Step 4: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206`. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:
   - If `parsed_url.scheme == "file"`:
     - **Reject** if any component of `Path(parsed_url.path).parts` equals `".."` → `DownloadError`.
     - Resolve with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`; require `is_relative_to(_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve())`, `exists()`, `is_file()`.
     - On success: return the resolved path string without copying.
     - On failure: raise `DownloadError(f"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}")`.
   - Existing `http`/`https` branch unchanged.
2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))).expanduser()`. Read each call (no cache).
3. **Coverage**: `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` both delegate to `_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:248-277`); single change covers both.
4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` early-return alone; `file://` is handled before that branch.

### Step 5: Tests for the HTTP server (`tests/test_local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `tests/test_local_http.py`. Each test starts a `LocalHttpServer` on a tmp port (probe with `socket` until free), `materialization_dir=tmp_path / "mat"`, `file_ttl_seconds=3600`, `janitor_interval_seconds=99999` (effectively disable for handler tests), then drives `requests` against `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`.
2. **Cover**:
   - `test_health_no_auth_returns_ok`.
   - `test_ingest_missing_auth_returns_401`.
   - `test_ingest_wrong_token_returns_401`.
   - `test_ingest_valid_upload_returns_path` — multipart upload, returned `path` exists, `size` matches, file under `materialization_dir`, mode `0o600`.
   - `test_ingest_accepts_no_auth_when_auth_optional_true`.
   - `test_cleanup_rejects_dotdot_component_literal` → 400.
   - `test_cleanup_rejects_absolute_path_outside_dir` → 400; `Path.unlink` mock not called.
   - `test_cleanup_rejects_traversal_escape` → 400.
   - `test_cleanup_deletes_valid_file_returns_204`.
   - `test_cleanup_idempotent_on_missing_file` → second call 204, not 500.
   - `test_options_preflight_returns_cors_headers` — Allow-Headers covers `Authorization, Content-Type`.
   - `test_get_health_includes_cors_headers`.
   - `test_token_file_mode_is_0600_on_posix`.
   - `test_port_file_contains_bound_port`.

### Step 6: Tests for the janitor (`tests/test_janitor.py`)
**Scope:** Small — addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations

1. **Create** `tests/test_janitor.py`. Drive `_FileJanitor._sweep()` directly without a thread for deterministic assertions.
2. **Cover**:
   - `test_sweep_deletes_files_older_than_ttl` — create file, set mtime to `now - 7h` (TTL 6h), sweep, file gone.
   - `test_sweep_keeps_fresh_files` — fresh file (mtime = now), sweep, file present.
   - `test_sweep_keeps_files_at_exactly_ttl` — borderline (mtime = now - ttl), sweep, kept (use `>` not `>=`).
   - `test_sweep_skips_unrecognized_filenames` — drop a file named `random.txt` (doesn't match `<uuid>.<ext>` pattern); sweep; file still present even when stale (safety net for user-dropped files).
   - `test_sweep_handles_unlink_oserror_gracefully` — patch `Path.unlink` to raise `OSError`; sweep doesn't propagate.
   - `test_startup_sweep_cleans_stale_files` — start the janitor with `interval_seconds=99999`; immediately observe stale files were deleted by the startup sweep; then `stop_event.set()` and join.
   - `test_sweep_does_not_follow_symlinks_outside_dir` — create a symlink inside materialization dir pointing to a file outside; ensure janitor does not follow it (file outside is untouched).

### Step 7: Tests for `file://` handling and lifecycle gating
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`:
   - `test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir` — file NOT copied.
   - Same for `download_video_if_url`.
   - `test_rejects_file_url_outside_materialization_dir` → `DownloadError`.
   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_literal_dotdot_component`.
   - `test_rejects_file_url_with_traversal_escape` (`file:///etc/passwd`) → `DownloadError`.
   - `test_file_url_works_when_download_target_dir_is_none`.
2. **Create** `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`:
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_true_with_pat_only` → True.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_role_key_set` → False.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_key_alias_set` → False.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_with_anon_key_only` → False.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_auth_mode_service` → False.
   - Use `types.SimpleNamespace()` for `cli_args`.

### Step 8: Document the integration + security tradeoffs (no app-side code change)
**Scope:** Small

1. **In the deliverable summary**, enumerate:
   - **FOLLOW-UP A (auth, recommended long-term)**: shipped `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/ingest.ts` does not send `Authorization`. Long-term fix: app reads `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` and sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Short-term unblock: deployer sets `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` on the worker.
   - **SECURITY NOTE on auth-optional** (addresses FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2): `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` is **opt-out from the brief's documented threat model** (any local process on the same machine can hit `/ingest` and `/cleanup` to materialize files or delete legitimate files). It is intended for single-machine dev smoke testing only. The worker emits `[SECURITY]` warning logs at startup when the knob is enabled. Production worker setups MUST leave it unset; once FOLLOW-UP A Option 1 lands, the knob is no longer needed.
   - **FOLLOW-UP B (cleanup)**: not blocking. Worker runs a periodic janitor that deletes materialized files older than `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_FILE_TTL_SECONDS` (default 6h) every `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_JANITOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 30 min). Works for sibling tasks that share materialized inputs (`create-task/index.ts:502-504` + `maskedEditResolver:83-85` pattern), all failure paths uniformly, and crash recovery. The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays as a contract entry-point if the app ever wants to free a file early.
   - **FOLLOW-UP C (port, optional)**: app reads `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for non-default ports. Worker writes it; app currently uses hardcoded `8765` and we match.
   - **WITHDRAWN suggestions**: the v2 "edge function calls requestLocalWorkerCleanup" idea is mechanically incoherent (Deno hosted edge cannot reach `localhost`) and is withdrawn. The v3 per-task self-cleanup design is withdrawn because it breaks `num_generations`-style sibling tasks that share a `file://` input.
2. **Do not edit** the app side.

### Step 9: Run the test suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_local_http.py tests/test_janitor.py tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py -v` first.
2. **Run** the broader `pytest -x` to confirm no regressions.

## Execution Order

1. Land Step 1 (HTTP server) and Step 5 (server tests) together.
2. Land Step 2 (janitor) and Step 6 (janitor tests) together.
3. Land Step 4 (`file://` support) and Step 7's `test_download_utils_file_url.py` together.
4. Land Step 3 (lifecycle wiring + security warning log) and Step 7's lifecycle tests last.
5. Step 8 is writeup-only alongside the final summary.

## Validation Order

1. Targeted unit tests from Steps 5 + 6 + 7 first (cheapest signal, no GPU/wgp imports).
2. Full `pytest` last to confirm no breakage in adjacent suites.
3. Manual smoke (PAT-mode worker on 8765, `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` set so the shipped app can talk to it today, `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_FILE_TTL_SECONDS=60` and `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_JANITOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS=10` for fast verification): `curl /health` (200, CORS headers + `[SECURITY]` log line at startup), OPTIONS preflight (204), `curl -F file=@... /ingest` (200), wait 75s, observe materialized file unlinked by janitor. Cloud-mode and anon-key-only launches leave 127.0.0.1:8765 closed.

test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir(materialization_dir, tmp_path):\n+    source_file = materialization_dir / \"input.png\"\n+    source_file.write_bytes(b\"image\")\n+    target_dir = tmp_path / \"downloads\"\n+\n+    result = download_image_if_url(source_file.as_uri(), target_dir)\n+\n+    assert result == str(source_file.resolve())\n+    assert not target_dir.exists()\n+\n+\n+def test_download_video_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir(materialization_dir, tmp_path):\n+    source_file = materialization_dir / \"input.mp4\"\n+    source_file.write_bytes(b\"video\")\n+    target_dir = tmp_path / \"downloads\"\n+\n+    result = download_video_if_url(source_file.as_uri(), target_dir)\n+\n+    assert result == str(source_file.resolve())\n+    assert not target_dir.exists()\n+\n+\n+def test_rejects_file_url_outside_materialization_dir(materialization_dir, tmp_path):\n+    outside_file = tmp_path / \"outside.png\"\n+    outside_file.write_bytes(b\"outside\")\n+\n+    with pytest.raises(DownloadError, match=\"file:// path not allowed\"):\n+        download_image_if_url(outside_file.as_uri(), tmp_path / \"downloads\")\n+\n+\n+def test_rejects_file_url_with_literal_dotdot_component(materialization_dir, tmp_path):\n+    outside_file = tmp_path / \"outside.png\"\n+    outside_file.write_bytes(b\"outside\")\n+    dotdot_url = (materialization_dir / \"..\" / outside_file.name).as_uri()\n+\n+    with pytest.raises(DownloadError, match=\"file:// path not allowed\"):\n+        download_image_if_url(dotdot_url, tmp_path / \"downloads\")\n+\n+\n+def test_rejects_file_url_with_traversal_escape(materialization_dir):\n+    with pytest.raises(DownloadError, match=\"file:// path not allowed\"):\n+        download_image_if_url(\"file:///etc/passwd\", None)\n+\n+\n+def test_rejects_missing_file_url(materialization_dir):\n+    missing_file = materialization_dir / \"missing.png\"\n+\n+    with pytest.raises(DownloadError, match=\"file:// path not allowed\"):\n+        download_image_if_url(missing_file.as_uri(), None)\n+\n+\n+def test_rejects_non_file_url(materialization_dir):\n+    with pytest.raises(DownloadError, match=\"file:// path not allowed\"):\n+        download_image_if_url(materialization_dir.as_uri(), None)\n+\n+\n+def test_file_url_works_when_download_target_dir_is_none(materialization_dir):\n+    source_file = materialization_dir / \"input.png\"\n+    source_file.write_bytes(b\"image\")\n+\n+    result = download_image_if_url(source_file.as_uri(), None)\n+\n+    assert result == str(source_file.resolve())\n*** End Patch\n"

## Plan (verbatim)

# Implementation Plan: Local-worker HTTP endpoints + `file://` input handling

## Overview

Goal: enable the dormant local-worker fast-path. Build a stdlib HTTP server with `/health`, `/ingest`, `/cleanup` (PAT-mode only, bound to `127.0.0.1:8765`, CORS-enabled, Bearer-auth on the two state-changing endpoints); teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` paths inside the materialization dir; and **make cleanup actually fire via a periodic age-based janitor**, not via per-task hooks that break sibling-task sharing.

**Architecture decision driven by iter-3 critique** (resolves FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations):
The shipped app's `create-task/index.ts:502-504` copies the same `materialized_inputs` array onto every task returned by a resolver, and `maskedEditResolver:83-85` creates one queued task per `num_generations`. Multiple non-orchestrator sibling tasks share the same `file://` input. The v3 plan's per-task self-cleanup would delete that shared input after the first sibling completes, breaking the rest. Switching to a **periodic age-based janitor** fixes this in one move:
- Background daemon thread sweeps the materialization dir every N minutes (default 30 min) and deletes files whose mtime is older than the configured TTL (default 6 hours).
- All sibling tasks of a single `create-task` call finish well within 6h, so shared inputs survive long enough to be consumed.
- All failure branches (raw-result failure, unhandled exception, requeue exhaustion) are covered uniformly without per-branch hooking — addresses FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations.
- Orchestrator chains (travel/join/edit_video) typically finish within the TTL; if a chain runs longer, the worker can extend the TTL via env var.
- Sweep also runs ONCE at startup so files left from a previous crash get cleaned.

The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays as a contract entry-point (built, tested, idempotent, auth-gated) for any future browser-driven flow that wants to explicitly free a file early. Cleanup correctness no longer depends on it.

**Other course corrections from iter-3 critique:**
- **FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2** (auth tradeoff visibility): keep `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` (default off) for single-machine smoke testing, but make the tradeoff loud: emit an `headless_logger.warning(...)` line at startup whenever the knob is enabled, prefixed `[SECURITY]`, naming the brief's threat model and instructing production setups to leave it unset. Add the same warning to the deliverable summary.

Repo shape:
- Polling worker entrypoint: `source/runtime/worker/server.py:main()` (`server.py:248-836`); auth-mode resolver at `server.py:47-64`.
- URL fetcher: `source/utils/download_utils.py:_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:202-240`).
- Reference for `file://` handling: `source/media/structure/download.py:57-64`.
- Test framework: `pytest` (`pyproject.toml:150-158`); `requests` already a dep.

Constraints baked in: bind `127.0.0.1` only; refuse path-traversal AND literal `..` components; gate startup on PAT-mode; persist `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` (0600) and `~/.reigh-local-worker/port`; do NOT pull in heavyweight HTTP frameworks.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Add the local HTTP server module (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

1. **Create** `source/runtime/worker/local_http.py` exposing `start_local_http_server(*, materialization_dir: Path, port: int, worker_id: str, version: str, auth_optional: bool = False, file_ttl_seconds: int = 21600, janitor_interval_seconds: int = 1800) -> LocalHttpServer` and `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`.
2. **Implement** with `http.server.ThreadingHTTPServer` bound to `("127.0.0.1", port)` plus a `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` subclass.
3. **Endpoints**:
   - `GET /health` → `200 application/json` `{"ok": true, "worker_id": ..., "version": ...}`. No auth check.
   - `POST /ingest` → require `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time `hmac.compare_digest`) UNLESS `auth_optional=True`. Parse `multipart/form-data` with `cgi.FieldStorage` reading exactly one file part named `file`. Sanitize the upload's filename suffix via `re.search(r"\\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$", filename)`; default `.bin`. Write to `materialization_dir / f"{uuid4().hex}{suffix}"` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_EXCL, 0o600)`. Respond `200` with `{"path": "<abs>", "size": <bytes>}`.
   - `POST /cleanup` → same auth gate. Read JSON `{"path": "..."}`. **Validate path components**: if any part of `Path(payload_path).parts` equals `".."`, return `400` (literal `..` ban — addresses prior FLAG-005). Then resolve via `Path(path).resolve(strict=False)` and require `is_relative_to(materialization_dir.resolve())`. Outside → `400`. Doesn't exist → `204` (idempotent). Else `unlink()` + `204`.
   - `OPTIONS <any>` → `204` with CORS headers.
   - Any other path/method → `404`.
   - Override `log_message` to route through `headless_logger.debug`.
4. **CORS**: on every response, set `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, `Access-Control-Allow-Methods: GET, POST, OPTIONS`, `Access-Control-Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`, `Access-Control-Max-Age: 600`.
5. **Token + persisted state**: `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`. Ensure `~/.reigh-local-worker/` exists with mode 0700; write `auth-token` via `os.open(..., O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC, 0o600)`; write `port` (the actual `server.server_address[1]`) as plain text. On shutdown, best-effort delete both files.
6. **Materialization dir**: `Path.mkdir(mode=0o700, exist_ok=True, parents=True)`; on POSIX, `os.chmod(0o700)` if pre-existed (best-effort).
7. **Threading**: `serve_forever()` on a daemon HTTP thread; `shutdown()` calls `server.shutdown()`, `server.server_close()`, joins with 5-second timeout AND signals the janitor thread to exit (see Step 2).

### Step 2: Add the periodic age-based janitor (`source/runtime/worker/local_http.py`)
**Scope:** Small — addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations

1. **Add** a `_FileJanitor` class in the same module:
   - Constructor takes `materialization_dir: Path`, `file_ttl_seconds: int`, `interval_seconds: int`, plus a `threading.Event` `stop_event`.
   - `run()` method: at startup do one `_sweep()` immediately (clean up files left over from prior crashes); then loop `while not stop_event.wait(interval_seconds): self._sweep()`.
   - `_sweep()` method: iterate `materialization_dir.iterdir()`, skip non-files, compute `age = time.time() - file.stat().st_mtime`. If `age > file_ttl_seconds`, `unlink(missing_ok=True)`. Wrap each unlink in `try/except OSError` and log via `headless_logger.debug`. NEVER follow symlinks; use `Path.is_file()` (which returns False for symlinks pointing outside, since `iterdir` only returns immediate children of the resolved dir). Skip any file whose name doesn't look like our `<uuid>.<ext>` pattern (regex `^[a-f0-9]{32}\\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$`) — keeps the janitor safe if a user manually drops files into the dir.
2. **Run** the janitor on its own daemon thread, started inside `start_local_http_server`. Pass the same `stop_event` shared with the HTTP server's `shutdown()`. On `LocalHttpServer.shutdown()`, set the event, join the janitor thread with a 5-second timeout.
3. **Why this design satisfies all open cleanup flags**:
   - Sibling tasks sharing a `file://` input all finish within `file_ttl_seconds=6h` → janitor doesn't race them.
   - All failure branches (raw-result failure path `server.py:740-757`, unhandled-exception path `server.py:760-789`, exhausted-retry, orchestrator chains) are covered uniformly with zero per-branch hooks.
   - Crash recovery: stale files from a prior crash get swept on next startup.
   - Worker doesn't need DB queries to check sibling-task references.

### Step 3: Wire the server into the worker lifecycle (`source/runtime/worker/server.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Add** the helper near `_resolve_worker_db_client_key` (`server.py:47-64`):
   ```python
   def _is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, *, access_token: str | None) -> bool:
       service_key = os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY") or os.environ.get("SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY")
       if service_key:
           return False
       if os.environ.get("WORKER_DB_CLIENT_AUTH_MODE", "").strip().lower() == "service":
           return False
       return bool(access_token)
   ```
2. **Start** the server in `main()` after `_initialize_db_runtime` succeeds (`server.py:500`) and before `task_queue.start()`. Guard with `_is_local_worker_mode(cli_args, access_token=access_token)`.
3. **Resolve** env-driven config:
   - `port = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_PORT", "8765"))`
   - `mat_dir = Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))).expanduser()`
   - `auth_optional = os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL") in ("1", "true", "yes")`
   - `file_ttl_seconds = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_FILE_TTL_SECONDS", "21600"))` (6h default)
   - `janitor_interval_seconds = int(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_JANITOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS", "1800"))` (30min default)
4. **Bind** the returned `LocalHttpServer` to a local variable in `main()`, defaulting to `None`. In the existing `finally` block (`server.py:804-835`), guard with `if local_http_server:` and wrap shutdown in `try/except BaseException`.
5. **Bind-failure posture**: if `start_local_http_server` raises `OSError` (port-in-use), log `headless_logger.warning(...)` and continue without the HTTP surface. Polling worker stays functional in cloud-fallback mode.
6. **Log** at startup:
   - `headless_logger.essential(f"Local HTTP server listening on 127.0.0.1:{port}, materializing to {mat_dir} (TTL={file_ttl_seconds}s, sweep every {janitor_interval_seconds}s)")`
   - **If `auth_optional=True`** (addresses FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2): `headless_logger.warning(f"[SECURITY] REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1 — /ingest and /cleanup accept requests WITHOUT Authorization. The brief's localhost-attacker threat model is opt-out in this configuration. Production worker setups must leave this knob unset.")`
7. **Do NOT add per-task cleanup hooks to `server.py`** (the v3 hooks at `:737-738` and `:740-757` are removed from the plan). The janitor covers all paths uniformly.

### Step 4: Teach `download_utils.py` to honor `file://` (`source/utils/download_utils.py`)
**Scope:** Small

1. **Extend** the URL gate at `download_utils.py:205-206`. After `parsed_url = urlparse(...)`:
   - If `parsed_url.scheme == "file"`:
     - **Reject** if any component of `Path(parsed_url.path).parts` equals `".."` → `DownloadError`.
     - Resolve with `Path(parsed_url.path).resolve(strict=False)`; require `is_relative_to(_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir().resolve())`, `exists()`, `is_file()`.
     - On success: return the resolved path string without copying.
     - On failure: raise `DownloadError(f"file:// path not allowed: {file_url_or_path}")`.
   - Existing `http`/`https` branch unchanged.
2. **Add** module-level `_resolve_allowed_local_worker_dir() -> Path` returning `Path(os.environ.get("REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_DIR", str(Path.home() / ".reigh-local-files"))).expanduser()`. Read each call (no cache).
3. **Coverage**: `download_video_if_url` and `download_image_if_url` both delegate to `_download_file_if_url` (`download_utils.py:248-277`); single change covers both.
4. **Leave** the `download_target_dir is None` early-return alone; `file://` is handled before that branch.

**Scope:** Medium

Each test starts a `LocalHttpServer` on a tmp port (probe with `socket` until free), `materialization_dir=tmp_path / "mat"`, `file_ttl_seconds=3600`, `janitor_interval_seconds=99999` (effectively disable for handler tests), then drives `requests` against `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`.

### Step 6: Tests for the janitor (`tests/test_janitor.py`)
**Scope:** Small — addresses FLAG-002 / issue_hints-1 / scope / callers / FLAG-006 / correctness / all_locations

1. **Create** `tests/test_janitor.py`. Drive `_FileJanitor._sweep()` directly without a thread for deterministic assertions.
2. **Cover**:
   - `test_sweep_deletes_files_older_than_ttl` — create file, set mtime to `now - 7h` (TTL 6h), sweep, file gone.
   - `test_sweep_keeps_fresh_files` — fresh file (mtime = now), sweep, file present.
   - `test_sweep_keeps_files_at_exactly_ttl` — borderline (mtime = now - ttl), sweep, kept (use `>` not `>=`).
   - `test_sweep_skips_unrecognized_filenames` — drop a file named `random.txt` (doesn't match `<uuid>.<ext>` pattern); sweep; file still present even when stale (safety net for user-dropped files).
   - `test_sweep_handles_unlink_oserror_gracefully` — patch `Path.unlink` to raise `OSError`; sweep doesn't propagate.
   - `test_startup_sweep_cleans_stale_files` — start the janitor with `interval_seconds=99999`; immediately observe stale files were deleted by the startup sweep; then `stop_event.set()` and join.
   - `test_sweep_does_not_follow_symlinks_outside_dir` — create a symlink inside materialization dir pointing to a file outside; ensure janitor does not follow it (file outside is untouched).

### Step 7: Tests for `file://` handling and lifecycle gating
**Scope:** Small

1. **Create** `tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py`:
   - `test_download_image_accepts_file_url_inside_materialization_dir` — file NOT copied.
   - Same for `download_video_if_url`.

2. **Create** `tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py`:
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_true_with_pat_only` → True.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_role_key_set` → False.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_service_key_alias_set` → False.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_with_anon_key_only` → False.
   - `test_is_local_worker_mode_false_when_auth_mode_service` → False.
   - Use `types.SimpleNamespace()` for `cli_args`.

### Step 8: Document the integration + security tradeoffs (no app-side code change)
**Scope:** Small

1. **In the deliverable summary**, enumerate:
   - **FOLLOW-UP A (auth, recommended long-term)**: shipped `reigh-app/src/shared/lib/localWorker/ingest.ts` does not send `Authorization`. Long-term fix: app reads `~/.reigh-local-worker/auth-token` and sends `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Short-term unblock: deployer sets `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` on the worker.
   - **SECURITY NOTE on auth-optional** (addresses FLAG-007 / issue_hints-2): `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_AUTH_OPTIONAL=1` is **opt-out from the brief's documented threat model** (any local process on the same machine can hit `/ingest` and `/cleanup` to materialize files or delete legitimate files). It is intended for single-machine dev smoke testing only. The worker emits `[SECURITY]` warning logs at startup when the knob is enabled. Production worker setups MUST leave it unset; once FOLLOW-UP A Option 1 lands, the knob is no longer needed.
   - **FOLLOW-UP B (cleanup)**: not blocking. Worker runs a periodic janitor that deletes materialized files older than `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_FILE_TTL_SECONDS` (default 6h) every `REIGH_LOCAL_WORKER_JANITOR_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 30 min). Works for sibling tasks that share materialized inputs (`create-task/index.ts:502-504` + `maskedEditResolver:83-85` pattern), all failure paths uniformly, and crash recovery. The HTTP `/cleanup` endpoint stays as a contract entry-point if the app ever wants to free a file early.
   - **FOLLOW-UP C (port, optional)**: app reads `~/.reigh-local-worker/port` for non-default ports. Worker writes it; app currently uses hardcoded `8765` and we match.
   - **WITHDRAWN suggestions**: the v2 "edge function calls requestLocalWorkerCleanup" idea is mechanically incoherent (Deno hosted edge cannot reach `localhost`) and is withdrawn. The v3 per-task self-cleanup design is withdrawn because it breaks `num_generations`-style sibling tasks that share a `file://` input.
2. **Do not edit** the app side.

### Step 9: Run the test suite
**Scope:** Small

1. **Run** `pytest tests/test_local_http.py tests/test_janitor.py tests/test_download_utils_file_url.py tests/test_local_http_lifecycle.py -v` first.
2. **Run** the broader `pytest -x` to confirm no regressions.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/.megaplan-worktrees/local-worker-endpoints-20260505/.megaplan-worktrees/local-worker-codex-20260505/all-codex/tests/test_janitor.py\n+import os\n+import threading\n+import time\n+from pathlib import Path\n+from unittest import mock\n+\n+import pytest\n+\n+from source.runtime.worker import local_http\n+from source.runtime.worker.local_http import _FileJanitor\n+\n+\n+def _recognized_name(char: str = \"a\", suffix: str = \".bin\") -> str:\n+    return f\"{char * 32}{suffix}\"\n+\n+\n+def _janitor(materialization_dir: Path, *, ttl: int = 21600, stop_event: threading.Event | None = None) -> _FileJanitor:\n+    return _FileJanitor(\n+        materialization_dir=materialization_dir,\n+        file_ttl_seconds=ttl,\n+        interval_seconds=99999,\n+        stop_event=stop_event or threading.Event(),\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def test_sweep_deletes_files_older_than_ttl(tmp_path):\n+    target = tmp_path / _recognized_name(\"a\")\n+    target.write_text(\"old\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    old = time.time() - (7 * 60 * 60)\n+    os.utime(target, (old, old))\n+\n+    _janitor(tmp_path, ttl=6 * 60 * 60)._sweep()\n+\n+    assert not target.exists()\n+\n+\n+def test_sweep_keeps_fresh_files(tmp_path):\n+    target = tmp_path / _recognized_name(\"b\")\n+    target.write_text(\"fresh\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+\n+    _janitor(tmp_path, ttl=6 * 60 * 60)._sweep()\n+\n+    assert target.exists()\n+\n+\n+def test_sweep_keeps_files_at_exactly_ttl(tmp_path, monkeypatch):\n+    now = 100000.0\n+    ttl = 6 * 60 * 60\n+    target = tmp_path / _recognized_name(\"c\")\n+    target.write_text(\"borderline\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    os.utime(target, (now - ttl, now - ttl))\n+    monkeypatch.setattr(local_http.time, \"time\", lambda: now)\n+\n+    _janitor(tmp_path, ttl=ttl)._sweep()\n+\n+    assert target.exists()\n+\n+\n+def test_sweep_skips_unrecognized_filenames(tmp_path):\n+    target = tmp_path / \"random.txt\"\n+    target.write_text(\"manual\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    old = time.time() - (7 * 60 * 60)\n+    os.utime(target, (old, old))\n+\n+    _janitor(tmp_path, ttl=6 * 60 * 60)._sweep()\n+\n+    assert target.exists()\n+\n+\n+def test_sweep_handles_unlink_oserror_gracefully(tmp_path):\n+    target = tmp_path / _recognized_name(\"d\")\n+    target.write_text(\"old\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    old = time.time() - (7 * 60 * 60)\n+    os.utime(target, (old, old))\n+\n+    with mock.patch.object(Path, \"unlink\", side_effect=OSError(\"blocked\")):\n+        _janitor(tmp_path, ttl=6 * 60 * 60)._sweep()\n+\n+\n+def test_startup_sweep_cleans_stale_files(tmp_path):\n+    target = tmp_path / _recognized_name(\"e\")\n+    target.write_text(\"old\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    old = time.time() - (7 * 60 * 60)\n+    os.utime(target, (old, old))\n+    stop_event = threading.Event()\n+    janitor = _janitor(tmp_path, ttl=6 * 60 * 60, stop_event=stop_event)\n+    thread = threading.Thread(target=janitor.run, daemon=True)\n+\n+    thread.start()\n+    try:\n+        deadline = time.time() + 2\n+        while target.exists() and time.time() < deadline:\n+            time.sleep(0.01)\n+        assert not target.exists()\n+    finally:\n+        stop_event.set()\n+        thread.join(timeout=5)\n+\n+\n+def test_sweep_does_not_follow_symlinks_outside_dir(tmp_path):\n+    outside = tmp_path / \"outside.txt\"\n+    outside.write_text(\"outside\", encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    mat_dir = tmp_path / \"mat\"\n+    mat_dir.mkdir()\n+    symlink = mat_dir / _recognized_name(\"f\")\n+    try:\n+        symlink.symlink_to(outside)\n+    except (OSError, NotImplementedError):\n+        pytest.skip(\"symlinks are unavailable on this platform\")\n+\n+    _janitor(mat_dir, ttl=0)._sweep()\n+\n+    assert outside.exists()\n+    assert outside.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\") == \"outside\"\n*** End Patch\n"
}
